A tiny compiled C client that speaks the same Unix-socket line protocol should materially reduce external-controller latency while keeping the Perl screenshot helper for the complex screenshot decode/write workflow.

Change:
- Add `src/ipc_client.c`, a small C Unix-domain socket client installed/built as `vimbrowser-ipc`.
- Preserve the same socket path resolution order: `VIMBROWSER_IPC`, `VIMBROWSER_PROFILE_DIR`, the maintained wrapper profile, `XDG_STATE_HOME`, `HOME`, then `/tmp/vimbrowser/ipc.sock`.
- Delegate `vimbrowser-ipc screenshot ...` to `vimbrowser-ipc-screenshot`, first adjacent to the binary, then the repo helper, then `PATH`, preserving existing screenshot behavior.
- Add the helper to CMake/install-wrapper and have the benchmark harness use `binary.parent / "vimbrowser-ipc"` when present, falling back to `scripts/vimbrowser-ipc` otherwise.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 100 shell-client iterations. Three Perl-script fallback runs (temporarily hiding the built helper) vs four compiled-helper runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-helper-summary.jsonl`; full outputs saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-helper-{baseline,patch}-{1..4}.{json,txt}`.

| metric | Perl script fallback | compiled C helper | result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `ipc.shell_status_ms` median mean | ~1.659ms | ~0.559ms | ~66.3% faster |
| `ipc.shell_status_ms` mean mean | ~1.688ms | ~0.564ms | ~66.6% faster |
| `ipc.shell_tabs_ms` median mean | ~2.047ms | ~0.951ms | ~53.5% faster |
| `ipc.shell_tabs_ms` mean mean | ~2.070ms | ~1.010ms | ~51.2% faster |

Validation:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark`
- `cmake --build build-source -j12`
- `make BUILD_DIR=build-source install-wrapper` installed `~/.local/bin/vimbrowser-ipc` plus `~/.local/bin/vimbrowser-ipc-screenshot`.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS; screenshot path was exercised through the C helper delegation (`local.screenshot.active_ms` ~34.3ms).
- Final slim pass succeeded after benchmark processes exited.
- Runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled; disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit. This is a large shell/backend win for external automation while preserving the source Perl script as fallback and retaining the dedicated screenshot helper for non-hot screenshot workflows.

## Experiment 129: report live resource host metrics — SUCCESS/BENCHMARK

Hypothesis: the live suite now reports aggregate/per-site Resource Timing counts and byte totals, but future content-blocking/backend experiments also need to distinguish first-party pages with a small host set from pages dominated by third-party asset hosts. Recording resource host counts and third-party resource counts should make real-site comparisons more actionable without changing browser runtime behavior.

Change:
- Extend the live smoothness/resource probe to derive host counts from each `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` entry.
- Record `resource_host_counts`, deterministic top-10 `resource_top_hosts`, unique host count, third-party resource count, and unparsable resource count in each live page record.
- Emit aggregate `live.resources.unique_host_count`, `live.resources.third_party_count`, and `live.resources.unparsable_count` metrics plus per-site `live.site.<label>.resources.*` metrics.
- Include host/third-party counts in the human-readable live page summary.

Validation:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark` and removed `scripts/__pycache__`.
- `scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark --suite live --live-url https://github.com/ --live-url https://news.ycombinator.com/ --binary build-source/Release/vimbrowser --output performance-research/results/2026-05-22-live-resource-host-metrics-smoke.json`.
  - Smoke output included new aggregate keys such as `live.resources.unique_host_count` and `live.resources.third_party_count`, and per-site keys such as `live.site.01_github_com.resources.third_party_count`.
  - GitHub reported 148 resource entries, 4 unique hosts, and 141 third-party resources, with top hosts recorded (`github.githubassets.com` dominated).
  - Hacker News reported 6 resource entries, 1 unique host, and 0 third-party resources.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS.

Safety: runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G, and result data remained small (~5.7MB total).

Decision: keep and commit as benchmark infrastructure. This adds the missing host/third-party dimensions needed to evaluate future real-site resource and content-blocking experiments with per-site context.

## Build preflight: native IPC client SIZE_MAX include fix

Current HEAD was clean at `2f1782057e` but `cmake --build build-source -j12` failed because `src/ipc_client.c` used `SIZE_MAX` without including a header that defines it on this toolchain. Added `#include <stdint.h>` and rebuilt successfully. This was a compile-portability fix before continuing performance experiments, not a benchmarked performance change.


## Experiment 130: pass explicit IPC socket path in benchmark shell client env — SUCCESS/BENCHMARK

Hypothesis: `scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark` already knows the exact temporary profile socket path. After adding the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client, the shell-client benchmark still passed only `VIMBROWSER_PROFILE_DIR`, forcing each subprocess to append `/ipc.sock` and retain fallback path logic. Setting `VIMBROWSER_IPC` as well should make shell metrics reflect the fastest supported client override and reduce benchmark noise/tails.

Change:
- In `BrowserSession`, set `self.ipc_env["VIMBROWSER_IPC"] = str(self.profile / "ipc.sock")` alongside `VIMBROWSER_PROFILE_DIR`.
- Keep the profile env for compatibility and fallback behavior.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 100 shell-client iterations. Compared three explicit-socket runs against the compiled-client profile-env rows from Experiment 128. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-ipc-benchmark-explicit-socket-summary.jsonl`; full outputs saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-ipc-benchmark-explicit-socket-{1,2,3}.{json,txt}`.

Observations:
- Shell `status` median mean improved slightly (~0.559ms -> ~0.545ms) and p95 tightened (~0.664ms -> ~0.608ms).
- Shell `tabs` median mean improved more noticeably (~0.951ms -> ~0.906ms) and p95 tightened (~1.366ms -> ~1.009ms).
- This is benchmark/client-env infrastructure, not a browser-runtime change, but it better represents the documented fastest socket override for automation.

Validation/safety: `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark` passed and `scripts/__pycache__` was removed. Runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit. Future shell-client IPC comparisons should use the known socket path directly while preserving `VIMBROWSER_PROFILE_DIR` for fallback compatibility.

## Experiment 131: single buffered request write in C IPC client — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` helper still writes command arguments with multiple `write()` calls (for example `status` plus newline). Building the full command line first and issuing one robust `write_all()` might shave syscall overhead from the sub-millisecond shell IPC path.

Changes tested:
- Heap-buffer variant: allocate a command buffer with `malloc()`, join arguments plus newline, then call `write_all()` once.
- Stack-buffer variant: use a 4KiB stack command buffer for normal commands and fall back to heap allocation only for unusually large commands.
- Keep socket path resolution, response streaming, and screenshot delegation unchanged.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 100+ shell-client iterations. Three current multi-write baseline runs, four heap-buffer single-write runs, and four stack-buffer runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-single-request-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- No useful win over the current multi-write request path. Heap and stack variants moved shell `status` and shell `tabs` within noise, with no repeatable tail improvement.
- The C helper is already dominated by process startup and response transfer; shaving one tiny request syscall/allocation is not measurable enough to justify extra command-buffer code.

Validation/safety: variants built successfully and passed focused IPC runs. After the result, restored `src/ipc_client.c` to the simpler multi-write request path and rebuilt `build-source`. Runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep the single-request buffer variants. Keep the simpler compiled C IPC client request write path.

## Experiment 132: add local resource fanout benchmark page — SUCCESS/BENCHMARK

Hypothesis: live resource metrics are useful but depend on public-network variability. A deterministic local page with many same-origin subresources should provide a repeatable harness for future request scheduling, resource loading, and backend overhead experiments without relying on external sites.

Change:
- Extend the benchmark HTTP server with `/fanout*.html`, a light page that references 240 local 1x1 image resources.
- Add `local.resource_fanout.*` metrics to the local suite: open command time, observed completion, load event, and measured Resource Timing count via IPC JavaScript.
- Keep existing `/page` and `/heavy` behavior unchanged.

Validation/results:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark` and removed `scripts/__pycache__`.
- Two local-suite smoke/check runs saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-resource-fanout-benchmark-{1,2}.{json,txt}` plus `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-resource-fanout-check.txt`.
- The new metric reported exactly 240 resources for each run; representative medians were observed-complete ~758ms and load-event ~731ms for the 240-resource page.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS.

Safety: all runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit as benchmark infrastructure for future backend/resource-loading experiments.

## Experiment 133: bypass tracker-host parsing for localhost resources — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: the new local fanout benchmark issues hundreds of same-origin localhost resource requests. `ShouldBlockRequest()` still converts each request URL to a host and checks tracker lists. A fast path that returns false for `127.*`/`localhost` URLs before host parsing might improve deterministic local high-resource load timing and reduce content-blocker overhead for internal pages.

Change tested:
- In `ShouldBlockRequest()`, materialize the URL once and return false for `http(s)://127.*` and `http(s)://localhost` before calling `HostFromUrl()` and `IsTrackerHost()`.
- Keep stylesheet/font bypass and tracker-host logic unchanged for other URLs.

Focused benchmark: local suite with the 240-resource fanout page. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-localhost-content-blocking-fastpath-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- No repeatable win. Fanout load/observed-complete medians moved within noise across baseline and patched runs; one patched run was slower/noisier while others were close to baseline.
- The extra URL prefix checks are not justified by the current deterministic local benchmark; request handling/loading work dominates over the simple host extraction/tracker check.

Validation/safety: patched C++ built and ran through local benchmark iterations. After the result, reverted `src/browser_client.cc`. Runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `src/browser_client.cc`; keep the current content-blocker request path.


## Experiment 134: skip resource handler for allowed non-captured requests — SUCCESS

Hypothesis: after native network capture was made opt-in, the default `GetResourceRequestHandler()` still returned the browser client for every request so content blocking could decide in `OnBeforeResourceLoad()`. For normal allowed resources this kept CEF invoking no-op response/filter/load-complete callbacks that immediately found no capture record. If the request is known not to be blockable, returning `nullptr` from `GetResourceRequestHandler()` should skip those callbacks without changing capture-enabled diagnostics or tracker blocking.

Change:
- When `NativeNetworkCaptureEnabled()` is false, call `ShouldBlockRequest(request)` in `GetResourceRequestHandler()` and return `this` only for requests that the native content blocker would cancel.
- Return `nullptr` for allowed requests so CEF uses default handling without invoking the resource-handler callback chain.
- Leave capture-enabled behavior unchanged: capture mode still records every request and still runs `OnBeforeResourceLoad()` for content blocking.

Focused benchmark: local suite with the 240-resource fanout page. Three restored-current AB runs vs five patched runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-resource-handler-allowed-null-summary.jsonl`; full outputs saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-resource-handler-{current-ab,allowed-null}-*.{json,txt}`.

| metric | current median values | current mean/median | allowed-null median values | allowed-null mean/median | result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `local.resource_fanout.observed_complete_ms` | 766.4, 754.0, 761.3 | 760.6 / 761.3 | 750.5, 758.0, 746.9, 754.2, 759.2 | 753.8 / 754.2 | ~0.9% mean-of-medians faster |
| `local.resource_fanout.load_event_ms` | 719.7, 736.7, 724.6 | 727.0 / 724.6 | 728.4, 727.0, 724.4, 718.4, 727.2 | 725.1 / 727.0 | neutral/slightly faster mean |
| `local.open_page.observed_complete_ms` | 154.1, 179.6, 183.2 | 172.3 / 179.6 | 181.3, 176.5, 180.1, 178.9, 179.4 | 179.3 / 179.4 | neutral/noisy |

The maintained high-resource fanout page shows a small but repeatable observed-complete improvement while broader local metrics remain within normal noise. The change is also logically sound: with capture disabled there is no useful response/filter/load-complete work for allowed requests, and blockable tracker requests still receive a handler so `OnBeforeResourceLoad()` can cancel them.

Validation:
- `cmake --build build-source -j12`.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS.
- Live smoke with GitHub + Hacker News saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-resource-handler-allowed-null-live-smoke.{json,txt}`; both pages loaded and live resource/host metrics were recorded (GitHub 148 resources/4 hosts, Hacker News 6 resources/1 host).

Safety: all runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G, and result data stayed small (~7.4M).

Decision: keep and commit. This removes unnecessary resource-handler callbacks from the default allowed-resource path while preserving diagnostics capture and native content blocking semantics.

## Experiment 135: binary-search native tracker domains — SUCCESS

Hypothesis: after allowed non-captured resources skip the resource handler, blockable native-content-filter requests still test each host against a small tracker-domain list linearly. Keeping the list sorted and using binary search for the full host plus each parent domain should reduce request-filter work without changing which tracker hosts are blocked.

Change:
- Keep `kBlockedDomains` sorted lexicographically.
- Replace the linear `HostMatchesDomain()` scan with `std::binary_search()` checks for the exact host and then each parent domain after `.` separators.
- Preserve URL host parsing/lowercasing and all request-type exclusions.

Focused benchmark: local suite with the 240-resource fanout page. Three linear-scan baseline runs vs five binary-search patched runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-tracker-bsearch-summary.jsonl`; full outputs saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-tracker-bsearch-{baseline,patched}-*.{json,txt}`.

| metric | linear median values | linear mean/median | binary-search median values | binary-search mean/median | result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `local.resource_fanout.observed_complete_ms` | 756.3, 755.3, 756.3 | 756.0 / 756.3 | 754.1, 761.4, 726.7, 752.9, 754.3 | 749.9 / 754.1 | ~0.8% faster mean-of-medians |
| `local.resource_fanout.load_event_ms` | 727.6, 725.3, 724.3 | 725.7 / 725.3 | 715.7, 724.9, 711.8, 722.1, 719.9 | 718.9 / 719.9 | ~0.9% faster mean-of-medians |
| `local.open_page.observed_complete_ms` | 178.5, 183.0, 181.4 | 180.9 / 181.4 | 179.4, 181.3, 178.1, 207.4, 179.8 | 185.2 / 179.8 | neutral/noisy |

The absolute win is small, but this keeps the tracker check cost bounded by host-label count plus log2(domain count) and removes repeated suffix comparisons against every domain.

Validation:
- `cmake --build build-source -j12`.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS (`performance-research/results/2026-05-22-tracker-bsearch-check.txt`).
- Additional sorted-list local check saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-tracker-bsearch-sorted-check.txt`.
- Live smoke with GitHub + Hacker News after the final sorted list saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-tracker-bsearch-live-smoke-sorted.{json,txt}`; both pages loaded and resource/host metrics were recorded.
- The sorted list preserves the native blocker domains while making `std::binary_search()` correct.

Safety: runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G, and result data stayed small (~8.5M).

Decision: keep and commit. This is a small backend/request-filter improvement with simple semantics and clean validation.

## Experiment 136: exit native IPC client with `_exit(0)` — FAILED

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client closes the socket and returns from `main()` after streaming the response. Replacing the final `close(fd); return 0;` with `_exit(0)` might avoid a little C runtime process-exit overhead on the already sub-millisecond shell IPC path.

Change tested:
- At the successful end of `src/ipc_client.c`, call `_exit(0)` immediately after the response stream loop instead of closing the socket and returning normally.
- Keep all error paths, socket setup, command writing, response streaming, and screenshot delegation unchanged.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 120 shell-client iterations. Four current baseline runs vs four `_exit(0)` runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-exit-fastpath-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- No useful win. Baseline shell `status` median mean was ~0.535ms and `_exit(0)` was ~0.544ms; shell `tabs` median mean was ~0.914ms baseline vs ~0.937ms patched.
- Direct in-process IPC metrics were unaffected/noisy as expected for a shell-client-only change.
- The normal close/return path is simpler and not measurably slower.

Validation/safety: both variants built with `cmake --build build-source --target vimbrowser-ipc -j12` and completed focused IPC runs. After the result, restored `src/ipc_client.c` and rebuilt `build-source/Release/vimbrowser-ipc`. Runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `src/ipc_client.c`; retain the normal close/return path.

## Experiment 137: avoid host lowercase allocation for already-lowercase request URLs — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: after native tracker-domain lookup moved to binary search, `ShouldBlockRequest()` still allocates and lowercases the parsed host for every blockable resource-type request. Most browser request hosts are already lowercase, so returning a `std::string_view` host and only lowercasing when ASCII uppercase is present might reduce local fanout/content-blocker overhead.

Change tested:
- Make `HostFromUrl()` return a `std::string_view` into the URL string instead of an allocated lowercased string.
- Add a tiny ASCII-uppercase scan; call `IsTrackerHost(host)` directly for already-lowercase hosts, and lowercase only for uppercase hosts.
- Preserve exact/parent-domain binary-search matching and resource-type exclusions.

Focused benchmark: local suite with the 240-resource fanout page. Three current string/lowercase baseline runs vs four host-view patched runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-host-view-blocker-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- Resource fanout observed-complete was mixed: median mean ~754.8ms baseline vs ~755.0ms patched, while mean improved slightly/noisily (~755.5ms -> ~753.3ms).
- Resource fanout load-event regressed slightly: median mean ~719.6ms baseline vs ~721.9ms patched.
- Normal local page timing was neutral, and restore startup was noisier/worse in the patched set.
- The change adds extra uppercase scanning and more subtle string-view lifetime requirements for no clear maintained-benchmark win.

Validation/safety: patched source built and four local-suite runs completed through the xenv-safe benchmark harness. After the result, source was reverted, rebuilt/slimmed, and installed. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `src/browser_client.cc`; keep the simpler lowercased host string path.

## Experiment 138: pass exact Unix socket address length in native IPC client — SUCCESS

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client still calls `connect()` with `sizeof(struct sockaddr_un)`, which includes unused trailing bytes after the socket pathname. Passing the exact pathname socket-address length (`offsetof(struct sockaddr_un, sun_path) + strlen(path) + 1`) may shave a little kernel-side work and tighten sub-millisecond shell IPC latency.

Change:
- Include `<stddef.h>` for `offsetof()`.
- Compute `addr_len` from the actual socket path length after filling `sockaddr_un`.
- Call `connect(fd, ..., addr_len)` instead of passing the full `sizeof(addr)`.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 120 shell-client iterations. Four full-`sockaddr_un` baseline runs vs four exact-length patched runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-sockaddr-len-summary.jsonl`; full outputs saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-sockaddr-len-{baseline,patched}-{1..4}.{json,txt}`.

| metric | full addr mean of medians | exact addr mean of medians | result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `ipc.shell_status_ms` | ~0.549ms | ~0.538ms | ~1.9% faster |
| `ipc.shell_status_ms` mean | ~0.576ms | ~0.561ms | ~2.6% faster |
| `ipc.shell_tabs_ms` | ~0.919ms | ~0.905ms | ~1.5% faster |
| `ipc.shell_tabs_ms` mean | ~0.943ms | ~0.908ms | ~3.7% faster |

Direct in-process IPC metrics were unaffected/noisy as expected for a client-only change. Shell tails also tightened in the patched set.

Validation:
- `cmake --build build-source -j12`
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS (`performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-sockaddr-len-check.txt`)
- Runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled.
- Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit. This is a tiny but repeatable native IPC client improvement and is the more idiomatic pathname socket length for Unix-domain `connect()`.

## Experiment 139: bypass tracker lookup for numeric hosts — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: the local resource fanout benchmark uses `127.0.0.1`, and after tracker-domain lookup moved to binary search, `IsTrackerHost()` still checks the full host plus parent suffixes for numeric IP hosts that can never match the blocked-domain list. Detecting numeric/IPv6-literal-style hosts and returning false early might reduce fanout request-filter work.

Change tested:
- At the start of `IsTrackerHost()`, scan the host and return false if it consists only of digits, `.`, `:`, `[` and `]`.
- Preserve normal binary-search domain matching for all domain-like hosts, including digit-prefixed domains such as `2mdn.net`.

Focused benchmark: local suite with the 240-resource fanout page. Compared current binary-search tracker rows against four numeric-host-bypass runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-numeric-host-bypass-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- No useful fanout win. Observed-complete median mean worsened/noised from ~749.9ms current to ~754.3ms patched, and load-event median mean was neutral (~718.9ms both) with worse p95 in the patched set.
- Open command timing was neutral. Other local metrics were mostly noise.
- The added scan does not beat the already-small cost of a handful of binary searches for localhost-style hosts.

Validation/safety: patched source built with `cmake --build build-source -j12` and completed four local-suite benchmark runs. After the result, reverted `src/browser_client.cc`, rebuilt/slimmed, and reinstalled the wrapper. Runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted the numeric-host bypass; retain the simpler binary-search tracker-domain path.

## Experiment 140: add local blocked-resource fanout benchmark — SUCCESS/BENCHMARK

Hypothesis: the local fanout page measures the allowed-resource path, but native content blocking changes also need a deterministic blocked-request harness. A local page that references many resources on a known tracker hostname should exercise the blocker/cancel path without relying on public network behavior, because the native blocker should cancel those requests before external DNS/network work.

Change:
- Extend the benchmark HTTP server with `/blocked-fanout*.html`.
- The page emits 240 image URLs targeting `google-analytics.com:<local-port>/asset?...`, a blocked tracker domain.
- Add `local.blocked_resource_fanout.*` metrics for open command time, observed completion, load event, Resource Timing count, and local-server request count.
- Keep existing allowed `/fanout*.html` metrics unchanged.

Validation/results:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark` and removed `scripts/__pycache__`.
- Smoke output saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-blocked-fanout-benchmark-smoke.{json,txt}`.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS (`performance-research/results/2026-05-22-blocked-fanout-check.txt`).
- The smoke run showed blocked fanout completing quickly (~102ms observed-complete median, ~65ms load-event median), while `server_request_count` stayed at 1, confirming the cross-host image requests did not reach the local HTTP server.

Safety: all runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit as benchmark infrastructure. Future native blocker/backend experiments can now compare both allowed-resource fanout and blocked-resource fanout in the maintained local suite.

## Experiment 141: skip second tracker match in blocked resource handler — FAILED

Hypothesis: in the default no-network-capture path, `GetResourceRequestHandler()` now returns the resource handler only for requests already classified as blockable. `OnBeforeResourceLoad()` then calls `ShouldBlockRequest()` again before returning `RV_CANCEL`. For blocked-resource fanout, replacing that second parse/match with an immediate cancel when network capture is disabled might reduce blocked request overhead.

Change tested:
- In `OnBeforeResourceLoad()`, return `RV_CANCEL` immediately when `NativeNetworkCaptureEnabled()` is false.
- Preserve the existing `ShouldBlockRequest()` path when native network capture is enabled, because capture can attach the handler to allowed requests too.

Focused benchmark: local suite with the blocked-resource fanout page. Three current baseline runs vs four immediate-cancel patched runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-blocked-handler-cancel-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- No win. Blocked fanout observed-complete median mean worsened from ~96.6ms baseline to ~98.8ms patched, and load-event median mean worsened from ~66.3ms to ~68.5ms.
- Open-command timing was neutral, `server_request_count` remained 1, and allowed fanout slightly worsened/noised in the patched set.
- The second match is not the current bottleneck, and keeping the explicit `ShouldBlockRequest()` in `OnBeforeResourceLoad()` is more conservative if future handler attachment changes.

Validation/safety: patched source built and completed four local-suite benchmark runs. After the result, reverted `src/browser_client.cc`, rebuilt/slimmed, and reinstalled the wrapper. Runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted the immediate-cancel shortcut; retain the existing `ShouldBlockRequest()` check in `OnBeforeResourceLoad()`.

## Experiment 142: use `writev()` for single-argument native IPC commands — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client writes common one-word commands (`status`, `tabs`) as two small writes: command bytes and newline. A single `writev()` for `argc == 2` might remove one syscall and reduce the shell-client IPC floor without allocating a joined command buffer.

Change tested:
- Include `<sys/uio.h>` and add a single-argument `writev()` path for `argv[1]` plus `"\n"`.
- Keep the default no-argument `status\n` write and multi-argument fallback loops unchanged.
- Preserve robust fallback writes for interrupted/partial `writev()` results.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 120 shell-client iterations. Three current multi-write baseline runs vs four single-argument `writev()` runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-writev-single-arg-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- Results were noisy and not convincing enough to keep. Shell `status` median mean moved from ~0.564ms to ~0.540ms, but one baseline run was a clear outlier; excluding it makes the result essentially neutral.
- Shell `tabs` median mean moved from ~0.954ms to ~0.897ms, but patched values included a high outlier and p95 did not improve repeatably.
- Direct in-process IPC was unaffected/noisy, as expected for a client-only change.
- This overlaps with the earlier failed single-request-buffer experiment; the extra partial-`writev()` handling complexity is not justified by a sub-millisecond, noisy shell-client signal.

Validation/safety: both variants built with `cmake --build build-source --target vimbrowser-ipc -j12` and completed focused IPC runs through the safe xenv benchmark harness. After the result, restored `src/ipc_client.c` and rebuilt `build-source/Release/vimbrowser-ipc`. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `src/ipc_client.c`; retain the current simpler write path.

## Experiment 143: small response buffer for native IPC `status` — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client reserves a 256KiB stack response buffer even for tiny `status` replies (~288 bytes). Using a smaller 4KiB response buffer for no-argument/default `status` and explicit `status` commands might reduce the shell `status` floor while leaving large `tabs` responses on the 256KiB path.

Change tested:
- Factor response streaming into a small `stream_response()` helper.
- Use a 4KiB stack buffer when the command is default/no-arg `status` or `argv[1] == "status"`.
- Keep the existing 256KiB buffer for all other commands, including `tabs`.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 120 shell-client iterations. Three current 256KiB-buffer baseline runs vs four status-4KiB patched runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-status-buffer-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- Shell `status` medians were only trivially lower: median mean ~0.541ms baseline vs ~0.539ms patched, while mean was neutral/slightly worse (~0.545ms -> ~0.547ms) and p95 worsened (~0.597ms -> ~0.612ms).
- Shell `tabs` slightly worsened/noised even though it still used the 256KiB path, likely from helper/control-flow noise: median mean ~0.895ms baseline vs ~0.909ms patched.
- The extra branch/helper is not justified for a couple of microseconds of median-only signal.

Validation/safety: both variants built with `cmake --build build-source --target vimbrowser-ipc -j12` and completed focused IPC runs through the xenv-safe harness. After the result, `src/ipc_client.c` was reverted, `vimbrowser-ipc` rebuilt, and install-wrapper refreshed. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted the status-specific response buffer; retain the simpler single 256KiB response buffer path.

## Experiment 144: add mixed allowed/blocked resource fanout benchmark — SUCCESS/BENCHMARK

Hypothesis: the maintained local suite now has separate allowed-resource and blocked-resource fanout pages, but real pages often interleave first-party assets with third-party tracker requests. A deterministic mixed fanout page should help future content-blocking/backend experiments catch interactions between allowed request loading and blocked request cancellation.

Change:
- Extend the benchmark HTTP server with `/mixed-fanout*.html`.
- The page emits 240 image elements, alternating local `/asset?...` URLs with blocked `google-analytics.com:<local-port>/asset?...` URLs.
- Add `local.mixed_resource_fanout.*` metrics for open command time, observed completion, load event, Resource Timing count, and local-server request count.
- Keep existing allowed `/fanout*.html` and fully blocked `/blocked-fanout*.html` metrics unchanged.

Validation/results:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark` and removed `scripts/__pycache__`.
- Smoke output saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-mixed-fanout-benchmark-smoke.{json,txt}`.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS (`performance-research/results/2026-05-22-mixed-fanout-check.txt`).
- The smoke run showed mixed fanout between the two existing endpoints: ~403ms observed-complete median and ~365ms load-event median, with `server_request_count` exactly 121 (the document plus 120 allowed local assets), confirming the blocked half did not hit the local server.

Safety: all runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit as benchmark infrastructure. Future resource/blocking experiments can now compare allowed-only, blocked-only, and mixed deterministic fanout paths.

## Experiment 145: cache native IPC socket path length — FAILED

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client calls `strlen(path)` multiple times while validating/copying the Unix socket path and computing the exact `sockaddr_un` length. Caching the length once might shave a tiny amount from the sub-millisecond shell client path.

Change tested:
- Compute `const size_t path_len = strlen(path)` once.
- Reuse `path_len` for path-length validation, copying `sun_path`, and computing the exact Unix socket address length.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 120 shell-client iterations. Four current repeated-`strlen()` baseline runs vs four cached-length runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-path-len-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- No win. Shell `status` median mean worsened from ~0.534ms to ~0.557ms and p95 worsened. Shell `tabs` median mean was effectively neutral (~0.947ms both), with mixed means/tails.
- Direct in-process IPC was unaffected/noisy, as expected for a client-only change.
- The extra local variable/reordered code is not justified by the measured shell path.

Validation/safety: both variants built with `cmake --build build-source --target vimbrowser-ipc -j12` and completed focused IPC runs through the safe xenv benchmark harness. After the result, restored `src/ipc_client.c` and rebuilt `build-source/Release/vimbrowser-ipc`. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `src/ipc_client.c`; retain the current straightforward path-length code.

## Experiment 146: skip top-level-domain parent tracker lookup — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: `IsTrackerHost()` checks the exact host and then every parent suffix. Because the native blocked-domain list contains only multi-label domains, checking the final top-level parent (`com`, `net`, etc.) can never match. Breaking before that last lookup should remove one binary search from common allowed hosts while preserving exact matches such as `doubleclick.net` and parent matches such as `sub.doubleclick.net`.

Change tested:
- In the parent-domain loop, stop before looking up a parent suffix that contains no dot.
- Preserve the initial exact-host lookup and all multi-label parent lookups.

Focused benchmark: local suite after adding the mixed fanout page. Three current baseline runs vs four top-level-parent-skip patched runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-tld-parent-skip-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- Allowed fanout did not improve: observed-complete median mean worsened/noised from ~751.6ms to ~755.5ms; load-event mean was roughly neutral/slightly faster but had a high patched outlier.
- Mixed fanout was neutral/noisy: observed-complete median mean ~400.3ms baseline vs ~402.3ms patched, while load-event mean moved only ~1.4ms faster.
- Blocked fanout remained noisy with no repeatable win: observed-complete median mean ~98.5ms baseline vs ~96.8ms patched, but patched samples ranged from ~88ms to ~103ms.
- The final TLD lookup is too cheap relative to renderer/network scheduling and image load variance to justify even a small special-case branch.

Validation/safety: patched source built with `cmake --build build-source -j12` and completed four local-suite benchmark runs through the xenv-safe harness. After the result, reverted `src/browser_client.cc` and rebuilt `build-source/Release/vimbrowser`. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G; results stayed ~11M before pruning full per-run JSON/TXT artifacts.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted the parent-loop break; retain the simpler exact+all-parent binary-search path.

## Experiment 147: literal writes for common native IPC commands — FAILED

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client still computes `strlen(argv[i])` for one-word commands before writing command bytes and a trailing newline. Special-casing common commands with literal `"status\n"` and/or `"tabs\n"` writes might reduce shell-client latency by avoiding strlen and one small write path branch.

Changes tested:
- Variant A: special-case both `argc == 2 && argv[1] == "status"` and `argv[1] == "tabs"`, writing literal `"status\n"`/`"tabs\n"`.
- Variant B: special-case only `status`, guarded by `argv[1][0] == 's'`, so `tabs` mostly keeps the generic path.
- Keep socket handling, response streaming, and multi-argument command behavior unchanged.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 120 shell-client iterations. Four baseline runs vs four patched runs for each variant. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-literal-command-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- Variant A improved shell `status` median mean (~0.542ms -> ~0.526ms) but regressed shell `tabs` substantially (~0.855ms -> ~0.901ms median mean), so it is not acceptable for the common `tabs` path.
- Variant B did not reproduce the status win; shell `status` worsened/noised (~0.535ms -> ~0.544ms) and shell `tabs` also regressed slightly (~0.919ms -> ~0.933ms).
- Direct in-process IPC was unaffected/noisy as expected for a client-only experiment.
- The extra command-specific branches are not repeatably better than the generic tiny write loop.

Validation/safety: variants built with `cmake --build build-source --target vimbrowser-ipc -j12` and completed focused IPC runs through the safe xenv benchmark harness. After the result, restored `src/ipc_client.c` and rebuilt `build-source/Release/vimbrowser-ipc`. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `src/ipc_client.c`; retain the current generic command write path.

## Experiment 148: add local fanout invariants to benchmark checks — SUCCESS/BENCHMARK

Hypothesis: the maintained local suite now includes allowed, blocked, and mixed resource fanout pages, but `--check` only guarded older startup/tab/screenshot metrics. Future backend/content-blocker changes could silently break the deterministic fanout harness (for example blocked resources reaching the local server or allowed resources not loading) while still reporting CHECK: PASS. Adding conservative fanout timing/count invariants should make benchmark validation safer.

Change:
- Add generous p95 observed-complete thresholds for allowed, blocked, and mixed fanout pages.
- Add summary max/min checks for deterministic resource/server counts:
  - allowed fanout resource count must stay 240;
  - blocked fanout local-server request count must stay at most 1;
  - mixed fanout local-server request count must stay exactly 121 (document plus 120 allowed local assets).
- Keep existing startup/tab/screenshot/background-throttle checks unchanged.

Validation/results:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark` and removed `scripts/__pycache__`.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS (`performance-research/results/2026-05-22-fanout-check-guards.txt`).
- The check run confirmed the new invariants on the current build: allowed resource count 240, blocked server request count 1, mixed server request count 121, and all fanout p95 timings far below the generous guard thresholds.

Safety: run used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit as benchmark infrastructure. This makes future performance experiments less likely to accept a broken fanout/content-blocking benchmark path.

## Experiment 151: annotate live resource metrics with known tracker host counts — SUCCESS

Hypothesis: after adding live resource host/third-party metrics and local fanout content-blocking benchmarks, live diagnostics still did not directly show whether a public page is pulling from hosts covered by the native blocker table. Adding a diagnostic count of known tracker-host resources to the live suite should make future blocker/backend experiments easier to interpret without changing browser behavior.

Change:
- Add the native blocker domain set to `scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark` as `KNOWN_TRACKER_DOMAINS` for diagnostic classification.
- Add `resource_known_tracker_count` and `resource_known_tracker_hosts` to live smoothness records by matching each observed resource host and parent domains.
- Add aggregate and per-site metrics under `live.resources.known_tracker_count` and `live.site.*.resources.known_tracker_count`.
- Include the value in the live text report.

Validation:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark`.
- Live smoke on `https://monkeytype.com/` saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-live-known-tracker-metrics-smoke.{json,txt}`.
- The smoke recorded 29 resources, 6 unique hosts, 4 third-party resources, and 1 known-tracker resource (`cdn.intergient.com`).

Safety: benchmark-only instrumentation; no browser runtime behavior changed. The live smoke used normal benchmark/xenv-safe launch paths. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit. This gives future live diagnostics a direct signal for native blocker coverage and missed tracker-resource fanout.

## Experiment 152: increase native IPC client response buffer to 1MiB — FAILED

Hypothesis: with the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client, large shell `tabs` responses for 10,000 restored tabs may still need multiple `read()`/stdout-write loop iterations with the current 256KiB response buffer. Increasing the native client response buffer to 1MiB might reduce large-response shell latency.

Change tested:
- In `src/ipc_client.c`, change the response buffer from `char buffer[262144]` to `char buffer[1048576]`.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 10,000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 35 shell-client iterations. Three 256KiB baseline runs vs four 1MiB runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-read-buffer-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- Large shell `tabs` medians regressed: mean-of-medians ~2.886ms baseline vs ~2.973ms at 1MiB.
- Shell `tabs` mean also regressed slightly: ~3.027ms baseline vs ~3.052ms at 1MiB.
- Shell `status` median moved slightly lower (~0.577ms -> ~0.566ms), but mean/p95 were neutral to worse; the tiny status signal does not justify hurting large `tabs`.
- Direct in-process `tabs` was noisy and is not expected to depend on the shell client read buffer.

Validation/safety: both variants built with `cmake --build build-source --target vimbrowser-ipc -j12` and completed focused IPC runs through the xenv-safe benchmark harness. After the result, `src/ipc_client.c` was reverted, `vimbrowser-ipc` rebuilt, and install-wrapper refreshed. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted the 1MiB response buffer; keep the native IPC client's 256KiB response buffer.

## Experiment 153: report IPC-suite startup ready time — SUCCESS

Hypothesis: several backend experiments touch IPC server/client startup behavior, but the maintained IPC suite did not expose the initial browser IPC-ready time as a first-class metric. Adding that metric makes future startup/socket experiments easier to evaluate without digging into records.

Change:
- Add `ipc.startup.ipc_ready_ms` to the IPC suite from the already-recorded `startup_status.observed_elapsed_ms` value.

Validation:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark`.
- IPC smoke with 100 restored tabs saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-ipc-startup-metric-smoke.{json,txt}`.
- The smoke reported `ipc.startup.ipc_ready_ms` (~421.7ms for that run) alongside the existing IPC metrics.

Safety: benchmark-only instrumentation; no browser runtime behavior changed. The smoke used benchmark/xenv-safe launch paths, disk stayed `/` ~210G free and `/tmp` ~14G free, and coredumps stayed unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit. This adds a useful maintained metric for future IPC startup/server experiments.

## Experiment 154: create native IPC client socket with `SOCK_CLOEXEC` — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` client does not exec after connecting to the browser, but creating the socket with `SOCK_CLOEXEC` may still avoid a separate descriptor-inheritance setup path in libc/kernel code and reduce subprocess IPC latency. This is a C-client revisit of the older Python `SOCK_CLOEXEC` experiment under the now sub-millisecond native helper.

Change tested:
- In `src/ipc_client.c`, create the Unix socket with `socket(AF_UNIX, SOCK_STREAM | SOCK_CLOEXEC, 0)` instead of plain `SOCK_STREAM`.
- Keep socket path resolution, exact pathname address length, command write path, and response streaming unchanged.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 120 shell-client iterations. Four plain-socket baseline runs vs four `SOCK_CLOEXEC` runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-sock-cloexec-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- Shell `status` was effectively neutral: baseline median mean ~0.537ms vs `SOCK_CLOEXEC` ~0.536ms, with slightly worse patched mean/p95.
- Shell `tabs` regressed slightly: baseline median mean ~0.908ms vs `SOCK_CLOEXEC` ~0.915ms, with worse patched mean/p95.
- Direct in-process IPC moved noisily and is not relevant to this client-only change.
- The flag is semantically harmless, but the maintained shell metrics do not justify adding it as a performance change.

Validation/safety: both variants built with `cmake --build build-source --target vimbrowser-ipc -j12` and completed focused IPC runs through the safe xenv benchmark harness. After the result, restored `src/ipc_client.c` and rebuilt `build-source/Release/vimbrowser-ipc`. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `src/ipc_client.c`; retain the current plain `SOCK_STREAM` socket creation.

## Experiment 155: add subdomain blocked resource fanout benchmark — SUCCESS

Hypothesis: after switching native tracker matching to exact/parent-domain binary search, the maintained local blocked-resource fanout benchmark only covered exact blocked hosts such as `google-analytics.com`. A deterministic subdomain fanout page using `pixel.google-analytics.com` would protect parent-domain blocker semantics and provide repeatable timing coverage for subdomain tracker cancellation.

Change:
- Add `/subdomain-blocked-fanout*.html` pages to the benchmark HTTP server.
- Each page emits 240 image requests to `pixel.google-analytics.com:<local-port>`, which should be canceled by the native blocker through parent-domain matching before external DNS/network work.
- Add `local.subdomain_blocked_resource_fanout.*` metrics and local check thresholds/guards.

Validation:
- `python3 -m py_compile scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark`.
- `scripts/vimbrowser-benchmark --suite local --check ...` saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-subdomain-blocked-fanout-check.{json,txt}`.
- The check passed. Subdomain blocked fanout observed-complete p95 was ~101.6ms, resource count median 246, and server request count stayed 1, confirming the tracker subdomain resources were blocked before hitting the local server.

Safety: benchmark-only instrumentation; no browser runtime behavior changed. The check used benchmark/xenv-safe launch paths, disk stayed `/` ~210G free and `/tmp` ~14G free, and coredumps stayed unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: keep and commit as benchmark infrastructure. This gives future blocker/backend experiments deterministic coverage for parent-domain tracker matches, not only exact-host blocking.

## Experiment 156: bind IPC server socket with exact pathname length — SUCCESS

Hypothesis: the IPC server still passed `sizeof(sockaddr_un)` to `bind()`, while the native IPC client already uses the exact pathname socket-address length for `connect()`. Binding with `offsetof(sockaddr_un, sun_path) + socket_path.size() + 1` avoids copying/checking unused `sun_path` padding and may slightly reduce IPC startup readiness time.

Change:
- Include `<cstddef>` in `src/ipc_server.cc`.
- Compute the Unix socket bind address length from the actual pathname length and pass that to `bind()` instead of `sizeof(addr)`.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 100 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and shell iterations disabled to focus on startup/direct IPC noise. Five current `sizeof(sockaddr_un)` baseline runs vs five exact-length patched runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-ipc-server-bind-length-summary.jsonl`; full outputs saved as `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-ipc-server-bindlen-{baseline,patch}-*.{json,txt}`.

| metric | sizeof(addr) values | sizeof(addr) mean/median | exact-length values | exact-length mean/median | result |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| `ipc.startup.ipc_ready_ms` | 414.8, 427.7, 409.6, 422.9, 437.7 | 422.5 / 422.9 | 416.3, 415.3, 412.1, 413.0, 416.0 | 414.5 / 415.3 | ~1.9% faster mean; ~7.6ms lower median |
| `ipc.status_ms` median | 0.124, 0.096, 0.095, 0.123, 0.088 | 0.105 / 0.096 | 0.119, 0.117, 0.154, 0.070, 0.094 | 0.111 / 0.117 | noisy/non-target |
| `ipc.tabs_ms` median | 0.103, 0.166, 0.128, 0.134, 0.182 | 0.143 / 0.134 | 0.200, 0.163, 0.135, 0.110, 0.143 | 0.150 / 0.143 | noisy/non-target |

Validation:
- `cmake --build build-source -j12`.
- `./scripts/slim-cef-runtime.sh build-source/Release`.
- `make BUILD_DIR=build-source install-wrapper`.
- `make benchmark BUILD_DIR=build-source` => CHECK: PASS.

Safety: runs used benchmark/xenv-safe browser launch paths with browser core dumps disabled. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G, and result data stayed small (~10M under `performance-research/results`).

Decision: keep and commit. This mirrors the already-successful exact-length native IPC client connect path and gives a small, repeatable startup-ready improvement without changing IPC protocol behavior.

## Experiment 157: compile native IPC client with `-Os` — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: the compiled `vimbrowser-ipc` helper is now dominated by process startup and a small amount of socket I/O. Compiling just that target with `-Os` might reduce instruction footprint/startup work and improve sub-millisecond shell IPC latency.

Change tested:
- Add `target_compile_options(vimbrowser-ipc PRIVATE -Os)` in `CMakeLists.txt` for the native IPC client target only.
- Keep browser build flags and IPC client source unchanged.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 5 direct iterations, and 120 shell-client iterations. Two completed default-Release baseline runs vs two `-Os` runs; a third attempted pair was interrupted during surrounding process cleanup, so it was excluded. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-c-ipc-osize-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- The resulting `vimbrowser-ipc` binary size was unchanged in this build (`16984` bytes for both variants), so the flag did not produce the intended footprint reduction.
- Shell `status` was neutral/slightly worse (~0.544ms baseline vs ~0.547ms `-Os` median mean), and p95 worsened slightly.
- Shell `tabs` median moved lower in the small sample (~0.932ms -> ~0.904ms), but mean was neutral and p95 worsened; not enough to keep a target-specific compile flag.
- Direct in-process IPC was unaffected/noisy as expected for a client-build-flag-only change.

Validation/safety: both variants built with `cmake --build build-source --target vimbrowser-ipc -j12` and completed focused IPC runs through the safe xenv benchmark harness. After the result, restored `CMakeLists.txt` and rebuilt the default `build-source/Release/vimbrowser-ipc`. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `CMakeLists.txt`; retain the default Release flags for `vimbrowser-ipc`.

## Experiment 158: scan IPC command reads with `memchr()` before append — FAILED/MIXED

Hypothesis: `IpcServer::HandleClient()` appends each read chunk to a command string and then searches the accumulated string for `\n`. Since normal clients send one newline-terminated command per connection, scanning the read buffer with `memchr()` before appending could avoid an extra string search/copy and improve direct IPC command latency.

Change tested:
- In `src/ipc_server.cc`, use `std::memchr(buffer, '\n', n)` on each read chunk.
- If a newline is found, append only bytes before the newline and stop reading; otherwise append the full chunk.
- Keep the existing `TrimNewline()` call for EOF/CR cases and leave response handling unchanged.

Focused benchmark: maintained IPC suite with 1000 restored tabs, 20 direct iterations, and 100 shell-client iterations. Three baseline runs vs three `memchr()` runs. Summary saved in `performance-research/results/2026-05-22-ipc-server-memchr-summary.jsonl`.

Observations:
- Direct `status` improved in this small sample (~0.099ms -> ~0.074ms median mean), but direct `tabs` regressed (~0.293ms -> ~0.318ms median mean).
- Shell `status` regressed (~0.530ms -> ~0.548ms median mean) and shell `tabs` was mixed (~0.925ms -> ~0.905ms median mean, but mean/p95 worsened slightly).
- IPC startup readiness was neutral/worse. The semantic cleanup is not enough to justify the mixed hot-path results.

Validation/safety: patched source built with `cmake --build build-source -j12` and completed focused IPC runs through the safe xenv benchmark harness. After the result, restored `src/ipc_server.cc`, rebuilt/slimmed `build-source/Release/vimbrowser`, and left the installed wrapper unchanged. Disk stayed `/` ~210G free, `/tmp` ~14G free, coredumps unchanged at 4.0G.

Decision: do not keep. Reverted `src/ipc_server.cc`; retain the current append-then-find command read loop.
